# K110-S: checkpoint inventory and deletion PROPOSAL (2026-09-29)

**This is a proposal, not a deletion. Nothing on /flare was deleted, moved or changed.**
Each tier below needs the user's explicit approval before any action. Any deletion then follows the
deletion protocol at the end of this card.

Scope: `$S = /lus/flare/projects/UIC-HPC/khuss/msdelta`. The data comes from one read-only metadata walk
(`nice -n 19 ionice -c3 find $S -printf ...`, 338,706 entries, 2026-09-29 ~06:00 UTC). Sizes are allocated
blocks in GiB (1 GiB = 2^30 B). The walk does not follow symlinks. It found 19,030 symlinks: wandb logs, and
`data/stage0-cap150/raw/*.parquet`, which points into `huggingface/`.

## 1. Sizes

**Total measured: 3,655 GiB (3.57 TiB, about 3.9 TB).** The OPEN_QUESTIONS note from earlier today said "ours
3.2 TB", so either the usage has grown or the units differ (TB vs TiB).

## 2. How runs were classified

- **Job ID**: the `-NNNNNNN` suffix of the dir name. 72 dirs have no job ID (diag or test dirs such as
  allocfix, armcount, ddp*, sibling*, gridexact, pf-*, p1-stage0, quarantine, plus `*.log` files).
- **final**: `runs/<run>/final/`. Every finished run that has checkpoints also has
  `final/model.safetensors` (> 1 MB). I checked this for all 352 such runs, and none lacks it.
- **Optimizer states**: `optimizer.pt`, `scheduler.pt`, `rng_state*.pth`, `global_step*/` (DeepSpeed ZeRO
  shards and model states). No `final/` dir contains any of these, so finals are already model-only.
- **Referenced**: a word-bounded (`grep -w -F`) match of the run dir name in `sweeps/arms/*.txt`,
  `notes/*.md`, `results/`, `configs/` and `pbs/tools/`. That gives 233 referenced run names.
  - A second grep over the whole repo (excluding .git/.venv/.keys) found code references in
    `sweeps/upload_denoise_models.py`, `msdelta/eval/eval_checkpoint.py`, `pbs/preflight_denoise.py`,
    `pbs/rescoring.pbs`, `sweeps/plot_summary.py` and `pbs/diag/*.sh`. These count as referenced.
  - Mentions only in `pbs/logs/` and `wandb/` do not count.
- **Protected checkpoints**: every `<run>/checkpoint-N` path that appears anywhere in the repo.
  - 78 are listed in arm files: `sweeps/arms` c8c19 snapshots / oodval / nine20k / grouped100k_c7best*;
    runs 8860522 (cont*_ep01, checkpoints 300/600/900) and 8872141 (s050m same-mass reference,
    checkpoints 531 to 2655).
  - The 8860522 checkpoint-600 encoders are also the frozen spectrum encoders cited in
    `results/raw/finetune/align/*.json`.
  - 7 cited checkpoints no longer exist on disk (8851663 `checkpoint-1200/1347` in `ckpt_vs_final.json`;
    `sweep-lr2e4_es05_ep4_h512_b12-8840408/checkpoint-29072` in `eval_checkpoint.py`). An earlier prune
    probably removed them. Those references now point at nothing and could only fail at run time.
- **Excluded from every tier (K110-X)**:
  - Live jobs (`qstat -u khuss`, 2026-09-29 ~05:50):
    - 8876832 hps400m and 8876833 hps025m: 24 run dirs, 0.01 GiB so far, and their `.configs-*`.
    - 8877117 msdelta-k114prof and 8877118 msdelta-k119flex: Pairformer diag. I excluded all `runs/pf-*`
      (17.9 GiB incl. checkpoints) and `runs/p1-stage0`, which is the feeder's CHECKPOINT_DIR.
    - 8877125/8877126 msdelta-evalgrp: queued C20 scoring of `sweeps/arms/score_c20.txt`.
  - Every run whose `final` is listed in `sweeps/arms/*.txt`. Its `final/` is kept; only its unreferenced
    `checkpoint-*` dirs are proposed.
  - `pretrained/`, `data/`, `eval-data/`, `huggingface/`. Hidden `.configs-<job>` dirs are provenance and
    are kept.

**Totals.**
- Tier A: 1,525 GiB. K110a 1,055 + K110b 417 + K110c 31 + K110d 22. Choosing K110a-lite instead of K110a
  gives 1,251.
- Tier B: 0.
- Tier C: 340 GiB (K110f 328 + K110g 12).
- A + C: **about 1,865 GiB (1.8 TiB, about 2.0 TB)**. That is two thirds of runs/.
- K110h and K110i add 11 GiB. K110j (165 GiB) needs its own review.

### Resume and scoring implications (K110-R)

- Deleting a run's `checkpoint-*` dirs means that run can no longer be resumed or extended. It also removes
  the per-checkpoint (snapshot) evaluations used in C8/C19, which is why the arm-referenced checkpoints are
  protected.
- Most K110a checkpoints carry full optimizer states (781 GiB), so those runs can be resumed today.
  K110a-lite keeps the checkpoint weights for snapshot evaluation but gives up the ability to resume.
- K66-C live runs 8876832 (400m, 14 h) and 8876833 (25m, 10 h) resume from their own `checkpoint-*`. They
  are excluded, and must stay excluded after they finish until `k66_pipeline.sh` and `k66_yeast.sh` have
  scored their finals.
  - Their config initialises only from `pretrained/msdelta-*-production-01-checkpoint-540423`, so no other
    run dir is a resume or init source for them.
  - The earlier killed K66 IDs 8875260 and 8875263 have no run dirs on disk.
- The C20 finals in `sweeps/arms/score_c20.txt` (8873850, plus the 8872141 reference) are being scored now
  (k100_val/k100_test; msdelta-evalgrp 8877125/6 queued). K110c and the 8872141 part of K110d wait until that
  scoring has finished and its results are committed.
- The 8860522 cont*_ep01 checkpoint-300/600/900 encoders are the frozen spectrum encoders behind the
  alignment results and the rerank-psm / align-targets artifacts. They stay.
- FT21 hazard, relevant to anyone re-scoring after a prune: a contrastive `checkpoint-N/` root loads as a
  RANDOM encoder, so only `checkpoint-N/encoder` paths are valid there.

## 4. Deletion protocol (applies to every tier; NOTHING is deleted until the user approves each tier)

1. **Approval per tier.** The user approves each tier ID (K110a, K110b, ...) separately. Log each approval
   in notes/DECISIONS.md.
2. **Freeze and regenerate the list.** Rebuild the candidate list right before deleting, from a fresh
   `qstat -u khuss`, a fresh `find`, and a fresh grep of the repo (arm files may change).
   - Drop any run of a queued or running job.
   - Drop any run that is an arm `final`.
   - Drop any protected `checkpoint-N`.
   - Drop anything under pretrained/, data/, eval-data/ or huggingface/.
   - Drop symlinks. Use `rm` without following links; never `rm -rf` on a glob.
   - Only delete paths that match `^$S/runs/<run>/checkpoint-[0-9]+$` (tier A) or `^$S/runs/<run>$`
     (tier C), taken from an explicit list file.
3. **Triple-check the list and the code.** Check that every path exists, is a real dir (not a symlink), and
   is under `$S/runs/`. Check that for tier A its run has `final/model.safetensors`. Check that no path is a
   prefix of a kept path. Have three independent predicates agree: name regex, job-ID set, and
   referenced-set exclusion.
4. **Dry run.** Print the full `rm` list with per-item sizes and the sum. The user reviews it, and it is
   compared against this card's estimate.
5. **Test on a redundant copy.** Build a scratch copy of representative dirs under `test-scratch/`,
   including trap cases:
   - a live-job run
   - an arm-final run
   - a run with protected checkpoints
   - a symlink
   - a `.configs-*` dir
   - quarantine/
   - a pf-* dir

   Run the real delete script on the copy. Verify that only the intended paths vanished and that everything
   else is byte-identical (checksums before and after).
6. **Staged live deletion.** Delete 1 item, verify; then about 10, verify; then the rest. After each stage,
   check that the kept `final/` dirs and protected checkpoints are intact (spot-load one final), and record
   the space reclaimed (`lfs quota`).
7. **Record.** Record what was deleted (list file plus sizes) in notes/DECISIONS.md and notes/STATUS.md.
